Worksheet: Verb Be & Have

Name: _______________ Date: _______________

Keys: answer-keys/worksheet-grammar-be-have-KEYS.md

A — am / is / are

  1. I ___ fine.
  2. She ___ at home.
  3. They ___ students.
  4. We ___ not busy.
  5. ___ you okay? (question)
  6. He ___ not here.

B — have / has

  1. I ___ a phone.
  2. She ___ two sisters.
  3. They ___ not ___ time.
  4. ___ he have a book?
  5. We ___ Wi-Fi today. (affirmative)
  6. I ___ not ___ a car.

C — Write 6 sentences about your home

Use: am/is/are (3) + have/has (3)

D — Translate idea to English (write full sentence)

  1. من خوبم. → _______________________________________________
  2. او کتاب دارد. → _______________________________________________
  3. آیا وقت داری؟ → _______________________________________________

Dari ideas only — sentence must be English.

E — Speaking

Read all answers aloud once. □ Done

راهنمای همراه — چگونه انگلیسی را با کمک دری یاد بگیرید

هدف

شما زبان انگلیسی را برای آینده، کار آنلاین، و تحصیل می‌آموزید. این پوشش دری فقط برای فهمیدن دستورالعمل‌ها است. مهارت با تمرین انگلیسی ساخته می‌شود.

اصول

اصلمعنی
انگلیسی اول در تمرینجمله‌ها را انگلیسی بنویسید
دری برای توضیحوقتی گیر کردید، خلاصهٔ دری را بخوانید
۳۰–۴۵ دقیقه در روزبهتر از یک روز طولانی در ماه
بدون شرماستراحت طبیعی است؛ دوباره شروع کنید
امنیتصدا و عکس‌های تمرین را خصوصی نگه دارید

مسیر پنج مرحله‌ای

مرحلهزمان تقریبیشما می‌توانید
۰هفته ۱–۲الفبا، سلام، ۵۰ کلمه
۱ماه ۱–۳زندگی روزمره، حال ساده
۲ماه ۴–۸گذشته، آینده، نظر
۳ماه ۹–۱۴جملهٔ پیچیده، ایمیل رسمی
۴ماه ۱۵+انگلیسی حرفه‌ای و روان

شروع سریع

  1. QUICK-START.md (انگلیسی)
  2. آزمون تعیین سطح → 14-assessments.md
  3. برنامه ۱۲ هفته → study-plans/12-week-beginner.md

واژه‌های کلیدی انگلیسی

Englishدری
studyمطالعه کردن
practiceتمرین
grammarقواعد / گرامر
vocabularyواژگان
pronunciationتلفظ
listeningشنیدن
speakingصحبت کردن
readingخواندن
writingنوشتن
checkpointآزمون مرحله‌ای

بعدی: 01-overview-dari.md

خلاصه دری — Overview (ماژول ۰۱)

فایل انگلیسی: 01-overview.md

این نقشه راه برای کیست؟

زنان جوان (حدود ۱۸–۲۵ سال) در افغانستان که انگلیسی آمریکایی را در خانه، با موبایل، بدون معلم، یاد می‌گیرند. زبان مادری: دری.

پنج مرحله

  1. پیش‌مبتدی — الفبا، کلمات ضروری
  2. مبتدی — زندگی روزانه، زمان حال
  3. متوسط — گذشته، نظر، پاراگراف
  4. متوسط بالا — متن طولانی، ایمیل رسمی
  5. پیشرفته — استدلال، ارائه، نوشتار حرفه‌ای

امنیت

  • جای امن برای مطالعه
  • رمز قوی برای اپ‌ها
  • ضبط صدا را خصوصی نگه دارید
  • با غریبه اطلاعات شخصی شریک نشوید

۳۰–۴۵ دقیقه در روز

  • ۵ دقیقه: مرور کلمات دیروز
  • ۱۵ دقیقه: درس اصلی
  • ۱۰ دقیقه: تمرین / شنیدن
  • ۵–۱۰ دقیقه: بلند خواندن یا صحبت

اگر مطالعه قطع شد

برنامه را دو برابر نکنید. ۱۵ دقیقه امروز کافی است. فردا ادامه دهید.

قدم بعدی

  • 16-tracker.md — ثبت پیشرفت
  • 14-assessments.md — آزمون شروع
  • 02-beginner-foundations.md — شروع درس

همراه: 02-beginner-dari.md

خلاصه دری — Beginner Foundations (ماژول ۰۲)

فایل انگلیسی: 02-beginner-foundations.md

هدف

  • الفبای لاتین (A–Z)
  • ۱۰۰ کلمهٔ اول
  • عبارت‌های ضروری: سلام، تشکر، کمک
  • معرفی خود به انگلیسی

الفبا

۲۶ حرف. پنج حرف صدادار: A E I O U. روی موبایل کیبورد را English (US) کنید.

گروه‌های کلمه (هر روز ۱۰ کلمه)

  1. اشخاص: I, you, he, she, we, they…
  2. خانه: house, room, water, food, tea…
  3. افعال: go, come, eat, study…
  4. خوب/بد: good, bad, happy, sad…
  5. ادب: please, thank you

اعداد ۱–۲۰

one, two, three … twenty — برای زمان و سن لازم است.

گفتگوهای نمونه

  • Hello. My name is … — سلام، نام من … است
  • Thank you. — تشکر
  • I need help, please. — لطفاً کمک می‌خواهم
  • I do not understand. — نمی‌فهمم

اشتباه رایج

اشتباهدرست
فقط در ذهن خواندنبلند بخوانید
۱۰۰ کلمه در یک روز۱۰ کلمه در روز کافی است
مخلوط حروف دری در جملهٔ انگلیسیفقط حروف لاتین در تمرین انگلیسی

تمرین

  • exercises/02-beginner-drills.md
  • appendices/word-list-300.md
  • assets/worksheets/worksheet-alphabet.md

بعدی: 03-grammar-dari.md

خلاصه دری — Core Grammar (ماژول ۰۳)

فایل انگلیسی: 03-core-grammar.md

فعل بودن (am / is / are)

در دری گاهی «است» حذف می‌شود؛ در انگلیسی حذف نکنید.

Englishمثال
I amI am fine. — حالم خوب است
she isShe is at home.
they areThey are students.

سوال: Are you fine? — حالت خوب است؟

have / has (داشتن)

Englishمثال
I haveI have a phone.
she hasShe has time.
Do you have …?Do you have Wi-Fi?

زمان حال ساده (عادت و حقایق)

  • I study → من درس می‌خوانم
  • She studies → او درس می‌خواند (با s)
  • منفی: I do not (don't) …
  • سوال: Do you study?

there is / there are

  • There is a book. — یک کتاب هست
  • There are two windows. — دو پنجره هست

a / an / the

در دری مقالهٔ تعریف‌شده مثل انگلیسی نیست. تمرین کنید:

  • a book — یک کتاب
  • an apple — یک سیب
  • the internet — اینترنت (مشخص)

ترتیب کلمات

انگلیسی: فاعل + فعل + مفعول
I study English. ✓ — نه: Study I English ✗

تمرین و آزمون

  • exercises/03-grammar-drills.md
  • unit-quiz-03-grammar.md
  • appendices/dari-glosses.md

بعدی: 04-vocabulary-dari.md

چگونه کلمه یاد بگیرید

برای هر کلمهٔ جدید:

  1. انگلیسی
  2. معنی سادهٔ دری
  3. یک جملهٔ انگلیسی
  4. تلفظ (با صدای بلند)

همان روز از کلمه در جمله استفاده کنید.

روزکار
۱۱۰ کلمهٔ جدید
۲مرور روز ۱ + ۱۰ جدید
۴مرور همه
۷مرور همه

اپ Notes روی موبایل کافی است.

مسیر ۱۰۰۰ کلمه

ماههدف
۱۱۵۰ کلمه
۲۳۰۰
۶۸۰۰+

خلاصه دری — Pronunciation (ماژول ۰۵)

فایل انگلیسی: 05-pronunciation.md
راهنمای صدا و لینک: pronunciation-audio-guide.md

هدف

فهمیده شدن — نه لهجهٔ کامل آمریکایی.

جفت‌های آوایی (خیلی مهم)

Englishتفاوت
ship / sheepکوتاه i / بلند ee
live / leave
pat / fatلب / دندان

صدای th

  • thank — زبان بین دندان، هوا بیرون (نه ت یا س)
  • this — با صدا

stress (تأکید هجا)

در computer تأکید روی PU: com-PU-ter
تأکید غلط = کلمه غریب به گوش می‌رسد.

تمرین روزانه ۵ دقیقه

  1. سه جمله از درس را بخوانید
  2. ضبط صدا
  3. یک صدا برای فردا انتخاب کنید

امنیت

پوشهٔ خصوصی در موبایل؛ ضبط‌های قدیمی را پاک کنید.

  • pronunciation-audio-guide.md

خلاصه دری — Listening, Speaking, Reading (ماژول ۰۶–۰۸)

۰۶ Listening — شنیدن

انگلیسی: 06-listening.md

  • اگر ۶۰٪ clip را فهمیدید، سطح مناسب است.
  • روال: گوش دادن → دوباره → یادداشت کلمه → با متن چک کردن.
  • زیرنویس انگلیسی اول؛ بعد بدون زیرنویس.
  • clip را ۳ بار در هفته تکرار کنید.
  • دانلود برای روز بدون اینترنت.

تمرین: unit-quiz-06-listening.md · worksheet-listening-dictation.md

۰۷ Speaking — صحبت

انگلیسی: 07-speaking.md

  • از روز اول بلند صحبت کنید.
  • Shadowing: بعد از گوینده تکرار فوری.
  • Self-talk: روز خود را انگلیسی تعریف کنید.
  • اعتماد: building-confidence.md
  • روانایی: building-fluency.md

تمرین: unit-quiz-07-speaking.md

۰۸ Reading — خواندن

انگلیسی: 08-reading.md

  • Phonics: حرف → صدا (درس ۰)
  • حدود ۸۰٪ کلمه‌ها را بشناسید.
  • همهٔ کلمه‌ها را ترجمه نکنید — حدس بزنید.
  • ۱۰ داستان: graded-readings.md

تمرین: unit-quiz-08-reading.md

بعدی: 09-writing-dari.md

خلاصه دری — Writing (ماژول ۰۹)

فایل انگلیسی: 09-writing.md

ترتیب جمله

فاعل + فعل + مفعول — I study English.

بزرگ و کوچ نوشتن حروف

  • اول جمله
  • I همیشه بزرگ
  • نام و کشور: Afghanistan, Sara

پیام کوتاه (مثل چت)

Good morning. I am fine. I studied 30 minutes today.

ژورنال روزانه (۵ جمله)

  1. Today I …
  2. I felt …
  3. I learned …
  4. Tomorrow I will …
  5. I am grateful for …

پاراگراف

  1. جملهٔ موضوع
  2. دو جزئیات
  3. پایان / احساس

کلمات رابط

because — چون · but / however — اما · first, then, finally — اول، بعد، در آخر

چک‌لیست قبل از ارسال

  • حرف بزرگ در اول
  • نقطه در آخر
  • I بزرگ
  • بلند بخوانید — آیا طبیعی است؟

انگلیسی: 10-intermediate-growth.md

گذشته (past)

  • walked, studied (قاعده -ed)
  • went, saw, had (بی‌قاعده — هر هفته ۵ تا)

گذشته استمراری

I was studying when … — داشتم درس می‌خواندم وقتی …

آینده

  • will — تصمیم / قول
  • going to — برنامهٔ از قبل
  • I am meeting … — قرار مشخص

نظر

I think … because … for example …

رابط‌ها

first, then, finally, however, although

راهنمای هزارگی — Hazaragi Guide Introduction

English for Hazara Speakers — Learning American English from a Hazaragi Perspective

Welcome — خوش آمدید

This is the first comprehensive Hazaragi companion guide for learning English. Hazaragi is a rich, valid language variety spoken by millions of Hazara people, primarily in Afghanistan (Hazarajat region) and diaspora communities worldwide.

This guide recognizes:

  • Hazaragi is not "broken Dari" — it's a distinct dialect with its own phonology, vocabulary, and grammar
  • Hazaragi speakers face unique pronunciation challenges when learning English
  • Common Hazaragi words and expressions deserve direct translation, not only Dari bridges
  • Accent notes: "how we say it" in Hazaragi vs Standard Dari vs English

Who This Guide Is For

  • Hazara women and girls learning English at home
  • Speakers of Hazaragi (هزارگی) as first or heritage language
  • Learners who think in Hazaragi but read Dari script
  • Anyone who says "I speak Hazaragi differently than Dari speakers"

You are seen. Your language is valid. This guide is for you.

Hazaragi vs Dari vs English

FeatureHazaragiStandard DariEnglish
VowelsSimplified (e/i often merge)6-7 vowels12-15 vowel sounds
ConsonantsSome unique (like ř)Persian setNew sounds: th, w, v
Word OrderSOV (flexible)SOV (flexible)SVO (strict)
ArticlesNoneNonea, an, the required
Common Wordsmardim (people), aftow (sun)mardom, āftābpeople, sun

Key insight: Hazaragi and Dari speakers both learn English as a foreign language, but with different starting sounds and vocabulary. This guide addresses Hazaragi-specific challenges.

What's in This Guide

Pronunciation Guide

  • Hazaragi accent patterns
  • Vowel mergers (e/i, o/u)
  • Consonant differences (ř, ğ)
  • Word stress (Hazaragi often stress-timed differently)
  • Minimal pairs for Hazaragi speakers

Common Hazaragi-English Vocabulary

  • 200+ everyday Hazaragi words with English equivalents
  • Direct translations (not Hazaragi → Dari → English)
  • Common expressions and idioms
  • Family, food, daily life terms

Hazaragi vs Dari Differences

  • When Hazaragi and Dari diverge
  • False friends and common confusion
  • Which variety to use when reading Persian script materials
  • Code-switching strategies

Learning Notes for Hazaragi Speakers

  • Grammar challenges specific to Hazaragi L1
  • Cultural considerations
  • Study tips using Hazaragi thinking patterns
  • Identity & language pride while learning English

How to Use This Companion

If You Speak Hazaragi Fluently

  1. Read English lessons in the main book
  2. Come here when:
    • Pronunciation is confusing
    • You want Hazaragi translation (not Dari)
    • Grammar explanation needs L1 bridge
  3. Use vocabulary lists for direct Hazaragi ↔ English practice

If You Speak Both Hazaragi & Dari

  1. Choose which language feels more natural for thinking
  2. Use Dari companion for formal grammar explanations
  3. Use Hazaragi companion for pronunciation and daily vocabulary
  4. Mix as needed — you are bilingual!

If You Read Dari Script But Speak Hazaragi

  1. This guide uses Perso-Arabic script (like Dari)
  2. Pronunciation notes show Hazaragi differences
  3. Example: Written آفتاب (āftāb / sun) → Hazaragi speakers say aftow

Spelling & Transliteration Note

Hazaragi has no standardized orthography. Different writers spell words differently. This guide uses:

  • Perso-Arabic script where possible (هزارگی)
  • Modified Dari spelling with pronunciation notes
  • Latin transliteration for pronunciation clarity

Examples:

  • مردم → mardim (Hazaragi) vs mardom (Dari) = "people"
  • آفتاب → aftow (Hazaragi) vs āftāb (Dari) = "sun"
  • دریا → daryow (Hazaragi) vs daryā (Dari) = "sea/river"

If you spell it differently in your community, that's valid too. Focus on the sound and meaning.

Pronunciation Key for This Guide

SymbolSoundExample (Hazaragi)
alike a in "father"mardim
elike e in "bed"ketow (book)
ilike ee in "see"zindagí
olike o in "go"aftow
ulike oo in "food"owdu (he/she)
řrolled r (distinct in some Hazaragi)beřem
ğvoiced velar fricativebağ (garden)
khlike ch in German "Bach"khow (sleep)

A Note on Language Pride

Hazaragi is not inferior to Dari or English.

  • Speaking Hazaragi at home while learning English is normal and good
  • Your accent in English will be influenced by Hazaragi — that's okay
  • Millions of native English speakers have accents too
  • Clear communication > perfect accent

Goals:

  1. Be understood in English (priority)
  2. Keep Hazaragi strong (identity)
  3. Use Dari when needed (written standard)
  4. Code-switch confidently (superpower)

You are multilingual — that's a strength, not a barrier.

Common Questions

Q: Should I learn Dari first, then English?

A: No. You can learn English directly from Hazaragi. This guide helps you do that.

Q: Will my Hazaragi accent hurt my English?

A: Every learner has an accent. Focus on clear pronunciation of key sounds (th, w, v). Hazaragi accent ≠ bad English.

Q: Can I use Dari materials if I speak Hazaragi?

A: Yes! Use Dari companion for grammar, Hazaragi companion for pronunciation and daily words. Mix freely.

Q: Why isn't there more Hazaragi content online?

A: Hazaragi is primarily oral and not standardized in writing. This guide is a step toward more resources. You can help by learning and teaching others.

Contributing Hazaragi Content

This guide is a starting point. Hazaragi varies by region (Bamyan, Daikundi, Ghazni, Quetta, etc.).

If you notice:

  • Missing vocabulary
  • Pronunciation differences in your region
  • Better explanations

You can help! See Contributing for how to suggest additions.

Start Learning

Ready to begin?

  1. Complete beginners → Start with main Beginner Foundations, refer here for pronunciation
  2. Pronunciation help → Hazaragi Pronunciation Guide
  3. Vocabulary → Common Hazaragi-English Words
  4. Grammar → Use main English chapters + Dari companion if needed

Remember:

Your language is beautiful. Learning another language makes you stronger, not less Hazara.

زبان شما زیبا است. یادگیری زبان دیگر شما را قوی‌تر می‌کند.

Hazaragi Pronunciation Guide for English Learners

Specific challenges and solutions for Hazaragi speakers learning American English

Overview

Hazaragi and English have very different sound systems. This guide identifies the key pronunciation challenges you'll face and gives you specific practice drills.

Good news: Once you train your ear and mouth for these sounds, English pronunciation becomes much clearer.

Vowel Challenges

Challenge 1: Hazaragi Vowel System is Simpler

Hazaragi vowels: ~4-5 main vowels (a, e/i, o, u) English vowels: 12-15 distinct vowel sounds

Impact: Many English word pairs sound the same to Hazaragi speakers at first.

English Vowel Pairs That Confuse Hazaragi Speakers

English PairHazaragi TendencyFix
ship /ɪ/ vs sheep /iː/Both sound like iship = short, relaxed; sheep = long, tense
cat /æ/ vs cut /ʌ/Both sound like acat = front, wide mouth; cut = central, relaxed
bet /ɛ/ vs bat /æ/Both sound like e or abet = mid; bat = low, open
pull /ʊ/ vs pool /uː/Both sound like upull = short; pool = long
cot /ɑ/ vs caught /ɔ/Both sound like o (merged in some US accents)Don't worry too much — many Americans merge these too

Practice Drill: Vowel Awareness

Week 1: Listen and repeat 10 times each day

  • ship — sheep (short i vs long ee)
  • sit — seat
  • hit — heat

Week 2:

  • cat — cut (front a vs central u)
  • bat — but

Week 3:

  • bet — bat (mid e vs low a)
  • pen — pan

Consonant Challenges

Challenge 2: English Consonants Not in Hazaragi

TH Sounds (Voiced & Voiceless)

Hazaragi: No th sound. Speakers often replace with t, d, s, or z.

English th types:

  1. Voiceless /θ/ — think, thank, math

    • Hazaragi speakers often say: tink, tank, mat
    • Fix: Put tongue lightly between teeth, blow air out (no voice)

  2. Voiced /ð/ — this, that, mother

    • Hazaragi speakers often say: dis, dat, mudder
    • Fix: Same tongue position, but add voice (feel vibration in throat)

Practice:

  • thank you (not tank you)
  • this is (not dis is)
  • mother (not mudder)

Drill: Say 20 times slowly every day for one week:

  • thank — that — think — this

W vs V

Hazaragi: w sound exists (و) but v is less common or borrowed.

English distinction:

  • W /w/: Round lips, no teeth — water, we, west
  • V /v/: Top teeth on bottom lip — very, vote, video

Hazaragi mistake: very → sounds like wery

Practice:

  • west (lips round) vs vest (teeth on lip)
  • wine vs vine
  • we vs V (letter)

Drill: Alternate 10 times:

  • very — west — video — water

R Sound

Hazaragi: Trilled or tapped r (like ř in some dialects) American English: Retroflex r (tongue curls back, doesn't tap palate)

Hazaragi mistake: red sounds like řed (with a trill/tap)

Fix:

  • Curl tongue back (tip doesn't touch roof)
  • Make a continuous sound: rrrrr (no taps)

Practice words:

  • red, right, run, mother, water, far

P, T, K (Aspiration)

Hazaragi: p, t, k are not strongly aspirated (no puff of air) English: Word-initial p, t, k have a puff of air

Test: Hold paper in front of mouth. Say pet — paper should move from air puff.

Hazaragi tendency: pet sounds like bet (no aspiration)

Fix: Exaggerate the puff of air at first

Practice:

  • pin, please, pretty
  • top, time, take
  • cat (= /k/), keep, come

Hazaragi-Specific Consonant Features

Hazaragi ř (Rolled/Trilled R)

Your advantage: You can roll r! This is harder for English speakers to learn.

For English: Don't roll it. Use American r (retroflex, no tongue tap).

Practice:

  • Hazaragi beřem (I go) → roll OK
  • English red → NO roll, tongue curled back

Hazaragi ğ (Voiced Velar Fricative)

Hazaragi: bağ (garden), dağ (mountain) have a soft, back-of-throat ğ

English equivalent: Like g in Spanish amigo (soft, not hard g)

English challenge: English doesn't have this sound. Closest is:

  • Soft g in get (but English g is a stop, not fricative)
  • Just use regular English g — it's fine

No drill needed — English g is simpler than Hazaragi ğ.

Word Stress & Rhythm

Hazaragi Stress Patterns

Hazaragi: Often stress-timed, but more flexible than English.

English: Strict stress patterns — wrong stress = confusing word.

English Stress Rules (Simplified)

  1. Two-syllable nouns: Usually stress first syllable

    • TA-ble, WA-ter, MOTH-er

  2. Two-syllable verbs: Often stress second syllable

    • be-GIN, re-PEAT, for-GET

  3. Longer words: Stress usually 2nd or 3rd syllable from end

    • pho-TO-graphy, com-PU-ter, edu-CA-tion

Hazaragi Mistake: Flat Stress

Hazaragi speakers often stress every syllable equally.

English: One syllable is LOUD and long; others are quiet and short.

Practice: Clap on the stressed syllable

  • com-PU-ter (clap on PU)
  • ENG-lish (clap on ENG)
  • to-MOR-row (clap on MOR)

Drill: Say these with exaggerated stress 10 times:

  • TEA-cher
  • STU-dent
  • im-POR-tant
  • be-CAUSE

Hazaragi Vowel Mergers in English

E / I Merger

Hazaragi: e and i often sound very similar (especially in fast speech)

English: They are distinct sounds.

English WordHazaragi TendencyFix
sit (short i)Sounds like setTongue higher, mouth more closed
seat (long ee)Sounds like sit or setTongue highest, longest vowel
set (short e)OKTongue mid-height

Practice:

  • sit — set — seat (all different!)
  • in — pen — seen

O / U in Unstressed Syllables

Hazaragi: o and u in unstressed syllables often merge

English: Both become schwa /ə/ (the lazy "uh" sound)

Example:

  • about → a-BOUT (not a-boot or a-boat)
  • sofa → SO-fuh (not so-fa clearly)

This is actually easier! English unstressed vowels are lazy too.

Sentence Rhythm & Intonation

Hazaragi Sentence Melody

Hazaragi: More melodic, rises and falls smoothly

English: Choppier — strong beats on content words, weak beats on function words.

English Sentence Stress Pattern

Strong (stressed): Nouns, verbs, adjectives, adverbs, question words Weak (unstressed): Articles (a, the), prepositions (in, on), pronouns (he, she, it)

Example:

I WANT to STUDY ENG-lish at HOME.

Not:

I WANT TO STUD-Y ENG-LISH AT HOME. (every word stressed — sounds robotic)

Practice: Say naturally, with rhythm:

  • She LIVES in KaBUL.
  • I NEED HELP with GRAM-mar.
  • WHERE is the BOOK?

Hazaragi-English Minimal Pairs (Unique)

These pairs are especially hard for Hazaragi speakers:

PairHazaragi ChallengeEnglish Fix
thin vs tinBoth sound like tinthin = tongue between teeth
van vs banBoth sound like ban or wanvan = teeth on lip
ship vs cheapBoth sound like shipcheap = add ch at start
very vs berryBoth sound like weryvery = teeth on lip for v
red vs ledMay sound similarred = tongue back; led = tongue forward

Drill (Week 1):

Practice these 5 pairs, 10 repetitions each, daily:

  1. thin — tin
  2. van — ban
  3. ship — sheep
  4. very — berry
  5. west — vest

Self-Recording Practice (Hazaragi Speakers)

How to Check Your Pronunciation

  1. Record yourself saying:

    Hello. My name is [name]. I study English at home. Thank you very much.

  2. Listen for these Hazaragi mistakes:

    • Did you say tank you instead of thank you?
    • Did very sound like wery?
    • Did you roll the r in English?
    • Was every syllable stressed equally?

  3. Fix one mistake at a time

  4. Re-record after 3 days of practice

Summary: Top 5 Hazaragi Challenges in English

  1. TH sounds (think, this) → tongue between teeth
  2. V vs W (very vs water) → teeth on lip for V
  3. Vowel pairs (ship vs sheep) → short vs long
  4. Word stress (com-PU-ter) → one syllable loud
  5. Final consonants (help, not hel) → finish the sound

Additional Resources

  • Main pronunciation guide: 05-pronunciation.md
  • Dari pronunciation (if helpful): companion-dari/05-pronunciation-dari.md
  • Audio guide: appendices/pronunciation-audio-guide.md
  • Drills: exercises/05-pronunciation-drills.md

Practice Plan:

  • Weeks 1-2: Focus on th sounds (daily 5 min)
  • Weeks 3-4: V vs W (daily 5 min)
  • Weeks 5-6: Vowel pairs (daily 5 min)
  • Ongoing: Word stress (every time you learn a new word, mark the stress)

Remember: Accent ≠ bad English. Goal = clarity, not perfection.

شو به‌خیر shaw bekheyrشب بخیر shab be kheyrgood night
خدا حافظ khodā hāfezخدا حافظ khodā hāfezgoodbye

Politeness

HazaragiEnglish
لطفاً lotfanplease
تشکر / ممنون tashakor / mamnōnthank you
عفو afuyou're welcome
ببخشید bebakhshīdexcuse me / sorry

Useful Expressions

HazaragiEnglish
نمی‌فهمم nemī-fahmamI don't understand
کمک می‌خوام komak mī-khwāmI need help
به‌انگلیسی چی می‌گیند؟ be engelīsī chī mī-gēnd?What is this called in English?
دوباره بگید dobāra begīdSay it again (please repeat)

Hazaragi-English Sentence Patterns

Present Tense

Hazaragi:

Man engelīsī mī-khōnam.

Word-for-word:

"I English [present marker]-read."

English:

"I study/read English."

Past Tense

Hazaragi:

Man dērōz ketow khāndom.

Word-for-word:

"I yesterday book read-[past]."

English:

"I read a book yesterday."

Future (using khāham / "I will")

Hazaragi:

Man fardā be maktab khāham raft.

Word-for-word:

"I tomorrow to school will go-[past participle]."

English:

"I will go to school tomorrow."

Study Tips: Hazaragi → English

  1. Don't translate word-for-word

    • Hazaragi: SOV (Subject-Object-Verb)
    • English: SVO (Subject-Verb-Object)
    • Example: "I book read" → "I read a book"

  2. Add articles (a, an, the)

    • Hazaragi has no articles
    • English requires them: "I have book" → "I have a book"

  3. English needs pronouns as subjects

    • Hazaragi: Mī-khōnam (verb alone, "I read/am reading")
    • English: I read (subject required)

  4. Hazaragi plurals are flexible; English plurals are strict

    • Hazaragi: mardim can mean "people" (already plural)
    • English: man → men, woman → women (irregular)

Regional Variations Note

Hazaragi varies across:

  • Hazarajat (Bamyan, Daikundi, Ghazni, etc.) — Central Afghan Hazaragi
  • Quetta (Pakistan) — Quetta Hazaragi has Urdu/Pashto influence
  • Iran — Iranian Hazaragi has more Persian influence
  • Diaspora — Mixed features

This guide uses Central Afghan Hazaragi as the baseline. If your words differ, they're still valid!

Practice Exercises

Exercise 1: Translate to English

  1. Man dar kor-am engelīsī mī-khōnam.
  2. Mār-am ketow mī-khōnad.
  3. Aftow besyār garm ast.

Answers:

  1. I study English at home.
  2. My mother reads a book.
  3. The sun is very hot.

Exercise 2: Translate to Hazaragi (try first, then check)

  1. I drink water every day.
  2. My brother goes to school.
  3. She is tired.

Suggested Hazaragi:

  1. Man har rōz āw mī-nōsham.
  2. Barār-am be maktab mī-berem.
  3. Ow khasta ast. (ow = she/he)

Next Steps

  • Expand vocabulary with Main Vocabulary Chapter
  • Learn English grammar (very different from Hazaragi): Core Grammar
  • Check Dari translations if needed: Dari Vocabulary

Hazaragi vs Dari: Key Differences

Understanding the relationship between Hazaragi, Standard Dari, and your English learning

Overview

Hazaragi and Dari are closely related but distinct language varieties within the Persian language family. Understanding the differences helps you:

  1. Read Dari-script materials while thinking in Hazaragi
  2. Avoid confusion when using Dari resources
  3. Feel confident that Hazaragi is valid, not "wrong Dari"

The Relationship

Persian Language Family
    ├── Dari (Standard Afghan Persian)
    ├── Farsi (Iranian Persian)
    └── Tajik (Tajikistan Persian)
        └── Hazaragi (Distinct dialect, closest to Dari)

Hazaragi is sometimes called:

  • A dialect of Dari
  • A separate language
  • Hazara Dari
  • Azaragi (older term)

What matters: It's your first language, and it's valid.

Key Linguistic Differences

1. Phonology (Sounds)

FeatureHazaragiStandard Dari
Vowel reductionCommon (āftāb → aftow)Less reduction
Final -ow / -awVery common (daryow, aftow)Final -ā or -āb (daryā, āftāb)
Vowel harmonySome patterns (regional)Less systematic
Consonant clustersSimplification commonMore consonant clusters retained
StressOften initial or penultimateMore varied

Example:

  • Dari: āftāb (sun) → Hazaragi: aftow
  • Dari: daryā (river) → Hazaragi: daryow

2. Vocabulary

Common Hazaragi Words Different from Dari

ConceptHazaragiStandard DariEnglish
Houseکور korخانه khānahouse
Morningسحر saharصبح sobhmorning
Peopleمردیم mardimمردم mardompeople
Sunآفتو aftowآفتاب āftābsun
Bookکتو ketowکتاب ketābbook

Shared Vocabulary

Most everyday words are the same or very similar:

  • zan (woman)
  • mard (man)
  • non (bread)
  • āw (water)
  • ketāb or ketow (book) — regional variation

3. Grammar

Verb Conjugation Differences

Example: "to go"

PersonHazaragi (Present)Dari (Present)
I goمیریم mī-beremمی‌روم mī-rawam
You goمیری mī-berīمی‌روی mī-rawī
He/She goesمیره mī-beraمی‌رود mī-rawad

Hazaragi simplifies the conjugation pattern.

Pronoun Dropping

Both Hazaragi and Dari allow pro-drop (omitting subject pronouns), but Hazaragi does it more frequently:

Hazaragi:

Mī-berem. ("Am going" — no "I")

Dari:

(Man) mī-rawam. ("I am going" — optional "I")

English:

"I am going." (Subject required!)

4. Syntax (Word Order)

Both Hazaragi and Dari use SOV (Subject-Object-Verb):

Hazaragi & Dari:

Man ketow mī-khōnam. (I book read.)

English uses SVO:

I read a book. (I read book.)

For English learners: You must switch your word order!

False Friends (Words That Look Similar But Differ)

WordHazaragi MeaningDari MeaningEnglish
شو shawNight(Different: shab)Night
سحر saharMorning(Different: sobh)Morning
کور korHouseBlind (in Dari, kor = blind)House

Watch out for: kor — in Dari it means "blind," but in Hazaragi it means "house"!

When to Use Dari vs Hazaragi

Use Hazaragi

  • ✅ Thinking and speaking at home
  • ✅ Talking to Hazara community
  • ✅ Internal monologue while learning English
  • ✅ Connecting with your identity and culture

Use Dari

  • ✅ Reading Afghan government documents (if needed)
  • ✅ Reading books/materials in Perso-Arabic script (most are Standard Dari)
  • ✅ Communicating formally in Afghanistan (Dari is official language)
  • ✅ Using Dari companion guides in this book (grammar explanations)

Use Both

  • ✅ Daily life in mixed communities (code-switching is normal)
  • ✅ Learning English (use whichever language helps you understand better)

Code-Switching Strategies

Code-switching = alternating between languages in conversation.

Many Hazara people switch between:

  • Hazaragi (home, community)
  • Dari (formal, written, official)
  • Pashto (if in mixed areas)
  • English (learning/professional)
  • Urdu (if in Pakistan/diaspora)

This is a superpower, not a weakness.

Example of Natural Code-Switching

Man dar kor-am English mī-khōnam. Har rōz vocabulary words mī-nivisam. It's difficult, amma mī-khām یاد بگیرم.

Translation:

"I study English at home. Every day I write vocabulary words. It's difficult, but I want to learn."

(Hazaragi + English + Dari mix — completely normal!)

Hazaragi Pride & Dari Respect

Hazaragi Is Valid

  • Hazaragi is not broken, incorrect, or inferior Dari
  • It's a natural linguistic variety with systematic patterns
  • Millions of people speak it
  • Oral literature, songs, and stories exist in Hazaragi
  • Your children can learn both Hazaragi and English

Dari Is Useful

  • Written materials in Afghanistan are mostly Dari
  • Learning Dari alongside Hazaragi is practical
  • English materials sometimes use Dari as L1 reference
  • Knowing both = more resources available

English Is Separate

  • Don't think of English as "Dari with different words"
  • English grammar is fundamentally different:
    • SVO word order
    • Articles required
    • Plural forms strict
    • Tense system different
  • Learn English directly, not through Dari

Practical Tips for Learning English as a Hazaragi Speaker

1. Think in Hazaragi, Learn in English

Don't do this:

Hazaragi → Dari → English (too many steps!)

Do this:

Hazaragi → English (direct association)

Example:

  • See a table → think mēz (Hazaragi) → say "table" (English)

2. Use Dari Resources When Helpful

This book has both Hazaragi and Dari companions.

  • Hazaragi guide: Pronunciation, daily vocabulary
  • Dari guide: Grammar explanations (more formal/written)

Use both as needed!

3. Don't Correct Your Hazaragi

Some Hazaragi speakers feel pressure to "fix" their Hazaragi to sound more like Dari.

For English learning, this doesn't matter.

  • Your goal: Hazaragi → English (fluent)
  • Not: Hazaragi → Dari → English (unnecessary)

4. Record Yourself in Hazaragi First

Before recording English practice, record yourself speaking in Hazaragi:

Name-am [name] ast. Man engelīsī mī-khām یاد بگیرم.

Then in English:

My name is [name]. I want to learn English.

Why? You'll see that accent = normal. Every language has its own sounds.

Summary Table: Hazaragi vs Dari vs English

FeatureHazaragiDariEnglish
Word orderSOVSOVSVO
ArticlesNoneNonea, an, the (required)
Vowels~4-5~6-7~12-15
Pronouns droppedVery commonCommonNever (subject required)
Verb conjugationSimplerMore complexDifferent system
Written formPerso-Arabic (unofficial)Perso-Arabic (official)Latin alphabet
Script directionRTL (right-to-left)RTLLTR (left-to-right)

Common Questions

Q: Should I learn Dari before English?

A: No! You can learn English directly from Hazaragi. This guide helps you do that.

Q: Will learning English make me forget Hazaragi?

A: No. Bilingualism/multilingualism strengthens your brain. Keep speaking Hazaragi at home.

Q: If I can't spell Hazaragi correctly, how can I learn English?

A: Hazaragi has no standardized spelling. English does have standardized spelling — you'll learn it step by step in this book. Totally separate skills.

Q: Is Hazaragi a "dialect" or a "language"?

A: Linguistically, it's a dialect of Persian. Socially and politically, language vs dialect debates are complex. What matters: Hazaragi is your valid first language. Treat it with pride while learning others.

Resources

  • Main book: Welcome
  • Hazaragi pronunciation: Pronunciation Guide
  • Hazaragi vocabulary: Common Vocabulary
  • Dari companion (if you prefer formal grammar explanations): Dari Guide

Remember:

Hazaragi ≠ wrong Dari English ≠ difficult Dari

All three are separate, valid languages/dialects.

You can learn English while staying proudly Hazara.

Hazaragi Learning Notes — Study Tips & Cultural Considerations

Specific guidance for Hazara women learning English

Identity & Language Learning

You Are Multilingual (Already!)

If you speak Hazaragi and understand Dari (even partially), you're already bilingual or bidialectal. Adding English makes you trilingual.

Benefits of being multilingual:

  • Faster pattern recognition
  • Better cognitive flexibility
  • Cultural bridge-building skills
  • More job/study opportunities

Your Hazaragi background is an asset, not a barrier.

Cultural Considerations

Learning English as a Hazara Woman

Historical Context

Hazara people have faced systemic educational barriers:

  • Discrimination and marginalization
  • Geographic isolation (mountainous Hazarajat)
  • Interrupted education (especially for girls)
  • Conflict and displacement

Result: Many Hazara girls had limited or no formal schooling — through no fault of their own.

Your Education, Your Terms

You don't need permission to learn.

  • Learning English is not disrespectful to Hazaragi culture
  • Education honors your ancestors (who valued knowledge)
  • English opens doors without erasing your identity
  • You can be proud Hazara and fluent in English

Balancing Tradition & Modernity

Traditional ValueHow It Supports English Learning
Respect for eldersLearn to communicate Hazara history and culture to the world
Community solidarityEnglish helps connect Hazara diaspora globally
Oral storytellingTranslate Hazara oral traditions; keep them alive
ResilienceLanguage learning requires the same perseverance Hazara people have always shown
Education as honorYour education honors your family, even if done privately

Learning English doesn't make you less Hazara. It makes you a Hazara woman with more tools.

Hazaragi Thinking Patterns & English Learning

Strength 1: Narrative Thinking

Hazaragi culture values stories (oral history, family tales, community memory).

Use this for English:

  • Learn vocabulary through stories, not lists
  • Practice speaking by telling simple stories: "Yesterday I…"
  • Read short English stories and retell them in your own words
  • Connect grammar to narrative: "If I go… then I will…"

Example Practice:

Think of a Hazaragi folk tale you know. Try to summarize it in simple English:

Long ago, there was a brave girl. She lived in the mountains. One day, she found a lost child. She helped the child find home.

Strength 2: Community Orientation

Hazaragi culture is collective, not individualistic.

Use this for English:

  • Study with a sister, cousin, or friend (even 15 min/day together)
  • Share vocabulary with someone else learning
  • Teach what you learn (teaching strengthens your own knowledge)
  • Think of English as a tool to help your community, not just yourself

Example:

"I learn English so I can translate health information for my mother."

Strength 3: Practical Skills

Hazara people are known for practical problem-solving (agriculture, crafts, trade).

Use this for English:

  • Focus on useful English first (health, safety, online work, communication)
  • Skip abstract theory; prioritize real-life skills
  • Learn English through practical activities: cooking (recipe vocab), shopping (money vocab), health (body parts, symptoms)

Overcoming Specific Challenges

Challenge 1: Limited Formal Schooling

Reality: Many Hazara girls had little or interrupted schooling.

Impact on English learning:

  • Unfamiliar with "school" learning style (grammar rules, exercises)
  • Reading in Perso-Arabic script may be weak
  • Latin alphabet is brand new

Solution:

  1. Start with spoken English — listening and speaking
    • You learned Hazaragi by listening; use the same method for English
  2. Treat Latin alphabet as a completely new system
    • Don't compare to Perso-Arabic; learn from scratch
    • Practice 10 min/day: Alphabet
  3. Use audio resources heavily
    • Podcasts, YouTube, voice apps
    • Listen → repeat → listen again
  4. Grammar comes later
    • Learn useful phrases first: "I need help." "Where is the bathroom?"
    • Add grammar rules gradually

Challenge 2: Shame About "Late Start"

Feeling: "I'm too old. I should have learned English in school."

Reality: Many successful English learners started as adults. Age is not the barrier — consistency is the key.

Solution:

  1. Reframe the narrative:

    • Not: "I'm behind."
    • Instead: "I'm starting now, which is brave."

  2. Compare to yesterday, not to others

    • Track small wins: "Last week I didn't know 'thank you,' now I do."

  3. Remember: Adults learn differently (better in some ways!)

    • Better discipline
    • Clearer goals
    • More life experience to connect to

Challenge 3: Juggling Home Responsibilities

Reality: Hazara women often have significant family/home responsibilities.

Impact: Hard to find 1-2 hours for study.

Solution:

  1. 30 minutes is enough
    • 15 min morning + 15 min evening = progress
  2. Integrate English into daily tasks
    • Listen to English while cooking
    • Label household items in English (stick notes: door, window, table)
    • Think in English: "I am washing dishes. The water is warm."
  3. Study when children study (if you have kids)
    • Parallel study time
    • Model learning behavior

Study Strategies Tailored to Hazaragi Speakers

Strategy 1: Use Both Languages in Notes

When learning new English words, write Hazaragi + English (skip Dari if it's not your natural thinking language).

Example vocabulary note:

EnglishHazaragiExample
waterآو āwI drink water — من آو می‌نوشم
sunآفتو aftowThe sun is hot — آفتو گرم است
houseکور korMy house is small — کور من کوچک است

Strategy 2: Hazaragi Sentence → English Sentence

Practice translating your own thoughts:

Hazaragi (think):

Man engelīsī mī-khām یاد بگیرم.

English (say aloud):

"I want to learn English."

Hazaragi:

Mār-am dar kor ast.

English:

"My mother is at home."

Do this 5 times/day with different sentences.

Strategy 3: Listening in Context

Hazaragi oral culture = strong listening skills

Use this:

  • Listen to English before reading transcripts
  • Guess meaning from context (like you do in Hazaragi conversations)
  • Replay short clips 3-5 times before looking up words
  • Mimic the speaker's rhythm and intonation

Recommended: BBC Learning English — slow, clear, free

Strategy 4: Record Yourself Daily

Hazaragi speakers: You're used to oral communication.

Practice:

  • Record 30 seconds in English every day
  • Topics: "My day," "What I ate," "The weather," "My family"
  • Listen the next day — note one thing to improve
  • Re-record the same topic after 1 week — hear your progress

Motivational Reminders for Hazara Learners

1. Your Ancestors Valued Knowledge

Hazara culture has always respected education and learning, even when access was denied.

Your English learning honors that tradition.

2. You're Not Alone

Millions of Hazara women globally are learning English, often in difficult circumstances.

Every word you learn, someone else is learning too.

3. Small Daily Steps = Big Results

Hazaragi proverb (adapted):

"آب قطره قطره دریا می‌شود" (Drop by drop, water becomes a river.)

English learning:

Word by word, you become fluent.

4. You Can Restart Anytime

Interruptions are normal.

  • War, displacement, family crises — these happen
  • English learning can wait when safety/survival is priority
  • The book will be here when you return

No shame in breaks. Only pride in returning.

Hazaragi-Specific Grammar Tips

Tip 1: English Needs Subject Pronouns

Hazaragi: Mī-berem. (verb alone = "I go")

English: Must say "I go" (subject required)

Practice: Add I, you, he, she, we, they to every sentence when translating from Hazaragi.

Tip 2: English Word Order is Strict (SVO)

Hazaragi: Flexible SOV

English: Must be SVO

Hazaragi thinking:

Man ketow (O) mī-khōnam (V). (I book read.)

English requires:

I (S) read (V) a book (O).

Practice: When translating from Hazaragi, rearrange: Subject first, then verb, then object.

Tip 3: Articles (a, an, the) Are Required

Hazaragi: No articles

English: Almost every noun needs a, an, or the

Hazaragi:

Ketow dar mēz ast. (Book on table is.)

English:

The book is on the table. (Articles added)

Practice: Every time you say a noun in English, ask: "Does it need a/an/the?"

Long-Term Goals: Why English Matters for Hazara Women

Personal Goals

  • Access global information (health, science, education)
  • Consume media and literature in English
  • Communicate with Hazara diaspora worldwide (many speak English as bridge language)

Professional Goals

  • Remote work (freelancing, virtual assistance, translation)
  • Online teaching (teach Dari/Hazaragi to others, earn income)
  • Healthcare, development, or international NGO work

Community Goals

  • Document Hazara history and culture in English (preserve and share)
  • Translate important information to/from Hazaragi
  • Advocate for Hazara rights and representation globally
  • Teach future generations (your children, nieces, community kids)

Political Goals (Long-Term)

  • Hazara women's voices in English-language media
  • English as a tool for activism and visibility
  • Countering stereotypes and misinformation about Hazara people

Your English is not just for you. It's for your people.

Resources Specifically for Hazara Learners

Hazara Organizations (Some Use English)

  • Hazara International Network
  • Hazara Diaspora groups (Facebook, WhatsApp)
  • Hazara Women's Network

Learning Recommendations

  • BBC Learning English — trusted, slow, clear
  • Duolingo — gamified, phone-friendly
  • EnglishClass101 — structured audio lessons
  • Voice of America (VOA) Learning English — news in simple English

Safe Social Media (If Accessible)

  • YouTube channels for English learners (no account needed)
  • Telegram groups for Hazara learners (be cautious, verify group safety)

Final Encouragement

You are a Hazara woman learning English in circumstances that are often difficult.

That makes you:

  • Brave
  • Resilient
  • A role model

Your English journey is not just language learning. It's resistance, survival, and hope.

Hazara proverb:

"تاریکی همیشگی نیست" (Darkness is not forever.)

English translation:

"Darkness is not forever."

Neither is ignorance. You are bringing light through learning.

May your learning journey be blessed.

Grammar

EnglishDari hintNote
a / anیک — general oneEnglish requires a book, not just book
theآن / همان — specificWhen both know which thing
am / is / areهست — but English never dropsWrong: I fine → I am fine
have / hasداشتنI have time = وقت دارم
do / does (questions)—Helper verb; Dari uses different question form
-s (he goes)—Third person always adds -s in present
there is / areوجود داردThere is a book = کتاب هست

Pronunciation

th (this)Voiced; vibrationthis, mother

p vs fپ vs فpat vs fat
short i vs long eeش vs شیship vs sheep

Word order

English patternDari habit
Subject – Verb – ObjectI study English

Blends (2–3 letters, one flow)

BlendExample wordsPractice sentence
shshe, wish, EnglishShe wishes to study English.
chchild, teach, muchShe teaches children.
ststudy, stop, listenStop and study.
strstrong, streetShe is strong.
brbrother, breadMy brother eats bread.
trtry, tree, studyTry to study.
plplease, planPlease plan time.
grgrateful, grammarI am grateful for grammar.

Week plan: One blend per day — Mon sh, Tue ch, Wed st …

Offline audio pack (no internet days)

On Wi-Fi, download:

  • 3 VOA 5-minute clips
  • 1 alphabet video (offline in YouTube app)
  • Your own voice memos Week 1–4

Store in folder: English audio offline

Mouth & voice tips

  • th: tongue visible between teeth lightly
  • v: bite lower lip gently
  • r (American): tongue not rolled; lips slightly round
  • Drink water — dry mouth hides sounds
  • Speak louder than whisper for practice

Link to Dari help

companion-dari/05-pronunciation-dari.md · dari-glosses.md

Building Confidence in English

Use with 07-speaking.md, 12-study-strategies.md.

Confidence is a skill

Confidence is not a gift. It is practice + kind self-talk + proof of progress.

You are allowed to sound like a learner. Learners become fluent.

7 confidence rules

  1. Speak before you feel ready — feeling follows action.
  2. One mistake per correction — not ten at once.
  3. Record in private — proof beats memory.
  4. Compare to last month — not to native speakers online.
  5. Celebrate speaking days — mark ✓ in tracker.
  6. Rest without guilt — return tomorrow.
  7. Your voice has value — ideas matter more than accent.

Shame recovery script (read aloud)

I stopped for a while. That is human. Today I study fifteen minutes. I am still a learner. I am still moving forward. I do not need permission to try again.

Write your own version in English. Read every Monday.

Weekly confidence tasks

DayTask
MonWrite 1 thing you did well in English
WedSpeak 5 sentences without reading
FriRecord 60 sec — delete if you want after listening
SunRead tracker wins from the week

Safe speaking environments

SafeLess safe
Alone in roomPublic criticism
Trusted sisterStrangers judging accent
Voice memo private folderPosting recordings publicly without reason

When someone mocks your English

You may say: I am learning. Then leave the conversation mentally. Do not carry their words into study time.

Confidence milestones

  • First recording kept 7 days
  • Spoke 5 days in one week
  • Introduced self without script
  • Handled one hard day without quitting 2 weeks
  • Taught one word to a sister

Pair with building-fluency.md.

Building Fluency

Use with 07-speaking.md, 10-intermediate-growth.md, 13-practice-plans.md.

What fluency means here

Fluency is smooth, understandable speech — not zero mistakes. You can be fluent at B1 and still learn until C2.

Fluency grows when your mouth practices English as often as your eyes.

The 4 fluency engines

EngineDaily actionMinutes
InputListen to clear English10
OutputSpeak aloud (self-talk)10
FeedbackRecord + listen once5
ReviewRepeat yesterday’s phrases5

Week 1–4 fluency challenge (beginner)

WeekChallenge
1Say 5 sentences about morning — no writing
2Shadow 10 lines from slow audio
360-sec voice note daily
4Describe a photo in 8 sentences

Week 5–12 (intermediate)

WeekChallenge
5–6Tell past story 90 sec
7–8Opinion + because + example 2 min
9–10Summarize a short article aloud
11–12Role-play both sides of a dialogue

Connector pack (paste in Notes)

First, … / Then, … / After that, … / However, … / Because, … / For example, … / In my opinion, … / Finally, …

Drill: Use 4 connectors in one 2-minute talk.

When you freeze

  1. Say: Let me think.
  2. Say idea in simple words.
  3. Use gesture if with sister (optional).
  4. Do not switch to Dari mid-sentence if goal is English mode.

Milestones

LevelFluency sign
A130 sec intro smooth
A21 min routine
B12 min story
B23 min opinion
C15 min structured talk

Track in 16-tracker.md — column S (speaking).

Exercise Sampler (PDF Page 39)

Try these samples. Full sets in exercises/.

Grammar: She ___ English every day. → studies

Have: I ___ a notebook. → have

Vocabulary: opposite of hot → cold

Pronunciation: practice pair → ship / sheep

Listening dictation: I study English at home every day.

Speaking: Say aloud: I am learning step by step.

Reading: Nadia studies ___ minutes. → thirty (see graded-readings)

Writing fix: hello my name is sara → Hello. My name is Sara.

Intermediate past: go → went

Advanced hedge: soften English is easy → English may seem easy at first.

Contributing and Expanding This Roadmap

Thank you for helping learners. This is a content repository (Markdown), not an app.

How to add content safely

  1. Keep American English spelling.
  2. Keep tone motivational and respectful for young women in Afghanistan.
  3. Avoid political campaigns, unsafe meetup advice, or shaming language.
  4. Do not put answer keys inside student modules — use answer-keys/.
  5. Add Dari glosses only in appendices/dari-glosses.md unless one line in a module is essential.

File conventions

TypeLocation
Core lesson0X-topic.md
Extra depthappendices/
Practiceexercises/
Answersanswer-keys/
Schedulesstudy-plans/

Rebuild the full PDF file

pnpm merge:all
# or separately:
pnpm merge          # ROADMAP-FULL.md (English)
pnpm merge:dari     # COMPANION-DARI-FULL.md
pnpm merge:worksheets  # WORKSHEETS-PACK.md

Then export per docs/PANDOC-GUIDE.md.

Suggested expansions

  • More graded readings in appendices/graded-readings.md
  • Audio links in 15-resources.md (URLs only)
  • Checkpoint quizzes with keys for new levels
  • Translations (separate branch/file — not mixed in English lessons)

Quality checklist before sharing

  • Links work on mobile Markdown viewers
  • Exercises tried without keys visible
  • Examples are culturally safe
  • ROADMAP-FULL.md rebuilt after large edits

Learners first. Perfection second.
